# ROUND 2/10

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
# Reasoning

## Understanding the Mission
- Score decreases by 1 for delivering the "good" item type, increases by 1 for delivering the "bad" item type
- One half of the source has BLUE walls → good items, the other has GREEN walls → bad items (or vice versa)
- We need robots to: go to the source, pick up good items (from BLUE or GREEN side), return to nest (WHITE floor), avoid the bad side

## Key Perceptions Available
- Floor color: BLACK (source), GRAY (transit), WHITE (nest)
- Camera: can see colored walls/blocks (BLUE=color 3, GREEN=color 2, RED=color 1, YELLOW=color 4, MAGENTA=color 5, CYAN=color 6)
- The RED wall is on the nest side

## Strategy
Since we don't know which side is "good" initially, I'll design FSMs that:
1. Explore toward the source (BLACK floor)
2. Try to bias toward one color (BLUE or GREEN) to pick up consistent items
3. Return to nest (WHITE floor / toward RED wall)

**Color codes:** RED=1, GREEN=2, BLUE=3, YELLOW=4, MAGENTA=5, CYAN=6

## FSM Designs

**Core behavior:** 
- State 0: Explore/move toward source
- State 1: Go toward BLUE wall (pick good items from BLUE side)  
- State 2: Return to nest (go toward RED wall)

Transitions:
- From exploration → go to BLUE when camera sees BLUE
- From exploration → return when on WHITE (nest, already delivered)
- From BLUE-seeking → return when on BLACK (picked up item)
- From return → explore when on GRAY (left nest)

I'll create variants with different color targets (BLUE vs GREEN), different navigation strategies, and different topologies.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I need to see the results from Round 2 to know which machines performed best. Since no scores were provided, I'll reason from first principles and diversify further.

## Reasoning

**Mission recap:** Score decreases by 1 for delivering the "good" item type. Robots must:
1. Navigate to source (BLACK floor)
2. Pick up items from the correct half (BLUE or GREEN wall side)
3. Return to nest (WHITE floor, toward RED wall)
4. Avoid/drop items from the bad side

**Key insight:** We don't know which color (BLUE=3 or GREEN=2) is "good." I'll hedge both, but also try asymmetric strategies where robots actively avoid one color side.

**Color codes:** RED=1, GREEN=2, BLUE=3

**Strategy variants:**
1. **Direct foraging (BLUE preferred):** Explore → GoToBlue (on black) → GoToRed (return)
2. **Direct foraging (GREEN preferred):** Same but GREEN
3. **Avoidance-based:** Go away from one color, toward the other
4. **Two-phase with re-routing:** Detect wrong item color, go back before returning
5. **Simple 2-state:** Just explore + return on white

I'll keep both BLUE and GREEN variants, try different probabilities, and add some with neighbor-based transitions for clustering effects.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I notice I'm not receiving score feedback between rounds, which means I need to reason more carefully about what should work theoretically and diversify aggressively.

## Reasoning

**Core problem analysis:**
- Robots need to go to BLACK floor (source), pick items from ONE color side only, return to WHITE floor (nest)
- The "good" side is either BLUE (color 3) or GREEN (color 2) - we don't know which
- The key is: after picking up an item (entering BLACK floor area), robots should navigate toward the correct colored wall, THEN return to nest

**Critical insight I may have missed:** The robot needs to be selective about WHICH part of the source it enters. If it enters the wrong half, it gets a bad item. So the strategy should be:
1. From gray/nest: seek BLUE (or GREEN) wall directly
2. When on BLACK under BLUE wall: return to nest (item collected)
3. At nest (WHITE): restart

**Another insight:** GoAwayColor from the bad color while seeking the source could help steer robots to the correct half.

**New topologies to try:**
- Direct navigation: GoToBlue → on BLACK → GoToRed → on WHITE → repeat
- Avoidance: GoAwayGreen while seeking source
- Simpler 2-state loops

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'm not receiving score feedback, which is a problem. Let me reason more carefully from first principles and try more diverse, well-reasoned designs.

## Reasoning

**Critical re-analysis of the mission:**
- Robots start in center (GRAY floor)
- Source = BLACK floor, two halves: one behind BLUE walls, one behind GREEN walls
- Nest = WHITE floor, RED wall on nest side
- One item type scores -1 (good), other scores +1 (bad)
- We must minimize score, so we want to deliver as many "good" items as possible

**The fundamental challenge:** We don't know if BLUE or GREEN is "good." But since we're running optimization, the best FSM will naturally favor the correct color. So I should submit BOTH variants and let the optimizer pick.

**What actually works mechanically:**
1. Robot must reach BLACK floor to get an item
2. Robot must then reach WHITE floor to deliver it
3. The item type depends on WHICH half of the source the robot entered

**Best strategy: Direct navigation**
- GoToBlue/GoToGreen: steers robot toward one half of source
- When on BLACK (item collected): GoToRed (toward nest wall = WHITE floor)
- When on WHITE (delivered): restart cycle

**Key fix:** The transition from "returning" state should trigger on WHITE floor (delivery), not GRAY. And the return journey uses RED wall (color 1) as beacon.

Let me also try: GoAwayGreen while seeking source (steers to BLUE half), and vice versa.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'm still not receiving score feedback, which means I need to think more carefully about what's fundamentally correct.

## Deep Reasoning

**Re-reading the rules carefully:**
- Entering a half of the source gives the robot an item of that half's type
- One half's item decreases score by 1 (GOOD), the other increases by 1 (BAD)
- We want to MINIMIZE score → deliver as many GOOD items as possible

**What sensors can distinguish the two halves?**
- The camera can see BLUE (3) or GREEN (2) wall blocks behind each half
- The floor under the robot when in source is BLACK regardless of which half
- So the robot must navigate TOWARD one color wall to enter that half

**The critical behavior loop:**
1. Navigate toward BLUE wall (or GREEN) → enter that half of source → get item
2. Navigate toward RED wall → reach WHITE floor → deliver item
3. Repeat

**Key issue I may have been missing:** When returning to nest, the robot should go toward RED (color 1) wall which is on the NEST side. When the floor becomes WHITE, delivery happens. Then restart.

**The transition logic:**
- In "seek source" state: when floor = BLACK → switch to "return to nest"
- In "return to nest": when floor = WHITE → switch to "seek source"  
- The WHITE→BLACK transition handles delivery automatically

**Alternative: use WHITE for return trigger instead of GRAY**

Let me also try completely different approaches: pure exploration with selective return, and attraction-based clustering.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'm still not receiving score feedback. Let me fundamentally reconsider my approach.

## Deep Reasoning

**What I've been doing wrong:** I keep submitting similar machines round after round without feedback. Let me think about what could actually be failing.

**The scoring mechanics again:**
- Entering BLACK floor gives item of that half's type
- Entering WHITE floor with item delivers it
- One color = -1 (good), other = +1 (bad)

**Critical realization:** The robot gets an item just by ENTERING the source area (BLACK floor). It doesn't need to navigate to a specific wall - it just needs to enter the correct HALF of the black area. The BLUE/GREEN walls are behind the halves, so GoToBlue/GoToGreen should steer the robot into the correct half BEFORE it steps on BLACK floor.

**But wait:** Once on BLACK floor, the robot already has the item. So the color-seeking should happen BEFORE reaching BLACK, to steer toward the correct half.

**Most promising structure:**
- State 0: GoToBlue (seeking BLUE wall → enters blue half of source → gets good item)
- Trigger: floor=BLACK → go to State 1
- State 1: GoToRed (return to nest)  
- Trigger: floor=WHITE → go to State 0

**This 2-state machine is the simplest correct structure.** Let me focus on this and variants.

**Also trying:** GoAwayGreen while approaching (steers toward BLUE half without directly targeting it).

**New insight:** Maybe the score has been POSITIVE (bad) because BLUE gives BAD items and GREEN gives GOOD items. I should try both orientations more systematically, and also try machines that explicitly avoid one color.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I still have no score feedback. Let me radically rethink.

## Radical Rethink

**What if the score is stubbornly positive (bad) because:**
1. Robots are delivering items from BOTH sides randomly → net score ≈ 0
2. Robots are stuck and not delivering anything → score = 0
3. Robots are systematically going to the wrong side → score > 0

**Key insight I've been missing:** The 2-state machine `GoToBlue → (on BLACK) → GoToRed → (on WHITE) → repeat` is mechanically clean BUT:
- GoToRed means going toward the RED wall (nest side) ✓
- On WHITE floor = delivered ✓
- Back to GoToBlue ✓

**But what if BLUE walls are NOT color 3?** Let me re-read: "wall blocks behind one half are BLUE and those behind the other half are GREEN." In ARGoS color encoding: BLUE=3, GREEN=2. That seems right.

**What if the robots can't actually see the wall colors from far away?** The camera detects colored objects. The wall blocks are colored, so they should be visible.

**New strategy: Try EVERY combination systematically:**
- GoToBlue vs GoToGreen for source navigation
- GoToRed vs GoAwayRed for return (GoAwayRed would push away from nest - WRONG)
- Different transition triggers (BLACK floor vs probability)

**Most importantly:** Let me try machines where the return is triggered by floor=BLACK (just entered source) with high probability, and also try floor=WHITE for restart.

**Also: What about a machine that just does random exploration and returns when it sees red?** Simple but might work.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Still no score feedback. Let me think completely differently.

## Complete Rethink

**The problem:** I've been submitting similar machines for 8 rounds with no feedback on what works. This suggests I need to be much more systematic and diverse.

**Fundamental question:** What if score=0 is what's happening (robots delivering equal good/bad items)? To get NEGATIVE scores (minimum), I need robots to deliver ONLY good items.

**What if I've been wrong about the navigation?** Let me reconsider:
- GoToColor moves toward the nearest object of that color
- The BLUE/GREEN wall blocks are at the FAR end of the arena
- The RED wall is at the NEST end
- So GoToBlue steers robots toward the BLUE half of the source ✓
- GoToRed steers robots back toward the nest ✓

**But what if the issue is that robots get stuck?** After entering BLACK floor and getting an item, if they're still pointing toward the BLUE wall, GoToBlue keeps them in the source. The transition ON BLACK floor to GoToRed is correct.

**What if the issue is the WHITE floor trigger for restart?** White floor = nest = delivery. After WHITE, robot should seek source again. But if GoToBlue is active and the nest is between robot and source, it might work fine.

**New hypothesis:** Maybe the robots ARE working but the score is just 0 because they deliver both good and bad items randomly. The key fix: make robots go AWAY from one color while approaching source.

**Let me try completely fresh topologies:**
1. Pure GoAwayGreen approach (avoids green half → enters blue half)
2. Pure GoAwayBlue approach (avoids blue half → enters green half)  
3. Exploration-only with return on white (baseline)
4. Multi-state with explicit color avoidance in source

**Also critical:** Try transitions on BLACK floor with probability < 1.0 to allow robots to penetrate deeper into the correct half before returning.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
This is the final round. I have no score feedback but must make my best attempt.

## Final Reasoning

**After 9 rounds of no feedback, I need to be maximally systematic.**

**The core foraging loop that MUST work:**
1. Navigate to source (BLACK floor) - specifically to one color half
2. Get item (happens automatically on BLACK)
3. Return to nest (WHITE floor) - delivery happens automatically
4. Repeat

**The simplest correct 2-state machine:**
- State 0: GoToColor X (X=BLUE or GREEN) → triggers on BLACK floor → State 1
- State 1: GoToRed (toward nest) → triggers on WHITE floor → State 0

**Why this should work:**
- GoToBlue/GoToGreen steers toward the correct wall → robot enters that half
- On BLACK = item collected → switch to return
- GoToRed = navigate toward RED wall (nest side) → robot reaches WHITE
- On WHITE = delivered → restart

**The score being minimized:** If BLUE=good, GoToBlue gives -1 per trip. If GREEN=good, GoToGreen gives -1 per trip. I must submit both.

**What else could help score?**
- More robots completing trips = lower score
- Faster trips = more deliveries = lower score
- No wasted time in wrong half

**For this final round, I'll focus on:**
1. The clean 2-state machines (both BLUE and GREEN variants)
2. GoAwayColor variants (avoid the bad half)
3. 3-state with brief exploration phase
4. Higher transition probabilities for faster cycling

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

The arena contains a nest area at one end, with a WHITE floor, and a source area at the opposite end, with a BLACK floor. The floor in between is GRAY. The wall on the nest side is RED.

The source area is divided into two halves. The wall blocks behind one half are BLUE and those behind the other half are GREEN.

A robot carries at most one item. Entering a half of the source gives the robot an item of that half's type, replacing any item it was already carrying. Entering the nest with an item delivers it: an item from one half decreases the score by 1, an item from the other half increases it by 1.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
